standalone replay, not a shuffled-peer claim. The original source audit also confirms SimGame.World

Aggregate minimum/desired/maximum compatibility reuses original CheckStockCapacity; distinct policies converging on the same endpoint/resource refuse rather than merge or delete intent. Independent buffers are not treated as aliases of local inventory. Changed backing with live original claims refuses. Same-key retained selector state cannot be reset to a conflicting declared choice. Ambiguous multi-choice or nonselection connected metadata still refuses explicitly; this slice does not guess those future bindings.

The original demand records retain entry/settings/hysteresis/created/dispatch history while destination, source identity and actual endpoint change together. No provider stock is copied or reset; historical flow receipts retain original endpoints. ### One contract-based engine protocol, actual provider-owned history

Extend original regional-change contracts with **ISpatialRegionChangeSource<TRegion>** and detached **SpatialRegionChangeSet<TRegion>**. Source facts declare the actual viewer version and either explicit full invalidation or a defensively copied readonly geometry list; they contain no view cursor, mutable provider, sample values or new state authority. The source version must equal that provider's actual viewer world snapshot. Provider geometry remains its own immutable, deterministically ordered representation; the generic engine does not invent tile geometry for other spaces.

New **SpatialRegionChangeView<TRegion>** is the sole cursor/observation protocol. It binds the actual selected source through explicit primary/interface registrations and existing same-owner alias rules; a foreign world's source refuses before Initialize. Reads use original EntityObservation over the actual source/space/clock, validate exact query-stream identity, publish initial full baseline without enumerating the world, and then validate actual source version and unchanged-empty semantics. Null/mismatched facts or full invalidation at an unchanged baseline fault, rather than becoming a fabricated successful reset. Original World first-fault/hash/save/clock cutoff remains authoritative.

Native **TileMap.ReadRegionChanges** converts its ORIGINAL chunk versions/full-reset data into detached rectangles. No chunk history is copied to the view. TileMapView's old cursor/chunk-projection algorithm is deleted and delegated to the shared query. Original chunk ordering/granularity, scope invalidation and map geometry remain unchanged.

Independent **SensorTileSpace** extends its EXISTING VisibleChanges owner with last-invalidated cell keys and full-reset baselines. Prepared sample observations capture exact per-viewer permitted keys, not one broad viewer list that could reveal hidden neighboring changes in a mixed batch. Actual source/owner revisions are preflighted while original resource books are settled; publication retains the existing transaction boundary. Repeated writes retain only each location's latest invalidation, not an event log or copied sample store. Scope acquisition/revocation clears prior cell invalidations at the new full baseline. Derived metadata is unsaved; restored queries establish a fresh stream over original saved samples/ownership/knowledge.

Non-tile **PointSpace** implements the SAME source contract and explicitly retains its existing coarse policy: actual viewer revision changes require full invalidation, while an unchanged baseline returns incremental-empty. This is provider-selected known granularity, not an unknown/always-full engine fallback. PointLayerView's old cursor protocol is deleted and delegated to the generic engine query; its separate page scope remains the original opaque page identity. Optional source capabilities are registered only when actually implemented—unsupported providers do not receive synthetic change data.

### One implementation for four tile query contracts

Extend existing **TileLayerView**, not another map/query owner. Its single Query/Read path now implements ISpatialLayerQuery and ISpatialLayerPagedQuery for integer and fixed values. Shared steps: selected actual metadata authorization, wide geometry validation, declared type, whole-buffer or page envelope, requested-sample authorization, opaque page identity/freshness, actual sample provider observation, exact conversion, and staged publication after original space/layer/clock guards verify. Byte/Int32 fractional/out-of-range refusals remain strict; no rounding, clamping or fake zero. All four explicit same-owner aliases resolve the same primary within that actual PlayerView projection.

Pages reuse **SpatialLayerPaging** and **SpatialChangeScope**, with the original layer/region binding and a transient per-query scope identity. The version comes from the actual provider's viewer-scoped world snapshot through original EntityObservation.ReadSpace, not global ObservationVersion or a new saved counter. Other viewers, sessions, restored projections, layers and regions cannot reuse the opaque cursor. A visible change returns Invalidated, while missing/private/hidden data refuses before revealing freshness. No cursor is published if a provider fails, mutates during observation or returns malformed values.

Whole-region queries and pages use one sample authorization and read loop. A page checks only its requested slice; hidden neighboring cells do not force enumeration or refusal of an authorized slice. Long row-major offsets and totals are retained; a page capacity is only a per-call bound, never a limit on world dimensions/area. Zero/terminal pages allocate no capacity-sized buffer and never divide by zero. The existing actual int.MaxValue-squared native-world case passes through the shared engine path.

Delete TileMapView's old native ResolveReadableLayer, CheckReadablePage, PageTile, sample-reading loops and concrete ByteLayer/IntLayer/FixedLayer page decisions. The native facade delegates both page contracts, as it already does ordinary reads. Its native chunk-based ReadChanges and other spatial facilities remain with their original owners; they are NOT silently replaced by generic full invalidation. This is engine simulation data, not Godot TileMap/TileMapLayer or rendering.

Extend existing embedded/master-listed **tile-query-types.json** with two rectangular regions, two knowledge facts, one layer-region visibility rule and one explicit forgetting policy. Existing sample extrema/world setup are unchanged. Extend original test Factory with an optional JSON policy-authoring callback; no new loader, visibility algorithm, provider or runtime default. Only the regional protocol fixture selects the authored rule; normal integer/sample cases retain their existing selected policy.

The native facade retains its own actual projection/query stream; cursors are not globally interchangeable across projection instances.

### Shared engine owner and contract-based composition

New Map/TileFixedLayerView implements existing **ISpatialLayerQuery<TileRect,Fix>** using actual ITileSpatialSpace, ISpatialLayerProvider and ITileSampleLayer. Both native TileSpaceDeclaration and independent SensorTileSpaceDef explicitly register that same framework implementation and interface with stable `tile-fixed.<authored-space-id>`/schema1. Native TileMapView's fixed regional method delegates to its same cached per-viewer contract; its old fixed-reading algorithm is removed. No implicit feature-discovery fallback, independent copy of privacy rules, native-map dependency in the independent space, new module or layer store. Existing typed feature registration already supports this interface; no registry rewrite or compatibility alias is needed.

Existing SpatialLayerAccess owns actual metadata/content/private/sample authorization. Existing EntityObservation verifies original space/layer/clock codecs around reading. Resolve private/missing identity before type/geometry, check region with wide arithmetic, preserve ValueTypeMismatch/InvalidRegion/empty-success behavior and per-IList result representability. This is not an engine world-size limit. Require actual authored/runtime layer metadata and sample capability; do not invent a default provider. Stage values locally and append only after complete source observation verifies, so a provider throw or mutation cannot publish the first sample. Read-only staging is not stock, a second balance or a cursor owner. TileSpatialGeometry.SampleSnapshot now owns the same detached tile-sample shape previously constructed in TileMap; both native visibility and shared query reuse it.

Architecture self-review rejects raw privileged driver reads and duplicated native/independent query algorithms. The existing public query contract plus explicit normal module composition gives one engine rule owner without expanding feature-lookup semantics. Integer and native paged/delta query paths are not claimed unified here; no renderer or platform map API is introduced.

SpillResourceAccountingTests.Spill now requests its authorized fixed sample through the public interface, requiring successful one-cell output instead of reading World.Content/Space. The normal source file contains no raw World access. Normal Dispose no longer invokes an effect directly; the preserved unchanged unfinished-effect assertions move into separately named native/independent negative protocol callbacks at genuine partial work. Both exact negative cases run the same physical flow and prove NotComplete cannot delete material or bypass time, then preserve normal continuation/replay. Actual player-raised source episode waits for delayed target exposure before3 of original5 liquid units enter the real private quantity layer. Physical incident/terrain cleanup cannot erase that stock. Machine recovery moves actual3 out of the layer into original inventory; real hauling loads/travels/unloads/departs. Disposal waits for real local delivery, earns partial four-minute work, respects native pause/disabled worker, preserves material on cancellation, then fresh complete work destroys3 exactly once through original attributed history/objectives. Separate idle worlds remain isolated. Six full physical continuations/replays pass; no driver stock, claim, incident, clock or completion writes are added.

Public query check covers native/independent owning/private/missing/type/invalid/empty facts, hash purity, one registered/cached interface, restored independent viewer model and native facade delegation. ### What is actually selected, and what is not established

Original P7.019 says **“Quality/lot fixture, if selected, shares one buffer and survives saved twin continuation.”** P7 explicitly makes these properties optional, not requirements of every game. Do not replace this with either mandatory lot tracking or a claim that plain inventory already implements it.

All448 native C# sources likewise have no candidates. Native reflection finds43 concrete module types and, across native/proof assemblies, three actual IResourceContentDefinition implementations: BasicResourceDef, DirectResourceContentDef and PlanetOreDef.

Lexical absence is not sufficient by itself: inspect IResourceDefinition/ResourceDeclaration/BasicResourceDef, independent DirectResourceContentDef, specialized PlanetOreDef and original ResourceInventoryState. The first two kinds expose authored units/phase/traits/capacity and stable resource identity; PlanetOreDef adds a required composition label, not per-lot identity. ### Actual engine defect, responsibility and retained guards

Legitimate SubmitPlan intent for original remote.upgrade is paused/saved, then resumed with an explicitly selected compatible worker. On core2.690 it stays Pending/no order/core.wrong-target forever. Native and independent runs each fail52/1; an added immutable plan diagnostic reproduces52/1 and shows the actual blocker. This is not a clock/worker/resource shortage or a reason to switch to an instant/direct-order path.

Cause: native EntityReplacementEffectDef.CheckStart calls full Upgrades.Check. The original WorkBoard reference owner sees the still-pending upgrade ITSELF, attempts to validate its old-prototype target as a surviving intent against the destination prototype, and rejects it. An earned active replacement already has an original same-order exception; activation had no corresponding exact-plan identity.

Repair stays in the same WorkBoard partial class. WorkPlans wraps native activation observations and activation callbacks with a call-local alias of the existing PlanEntry, restoring the previous alias in finally. WorkPlanReferenceReplacement classifies only that exact pending record/company/actual authored target and matching IEntityReplacementEffect source/destination as becoming its native execution, not a surviving intent needing rewrite. It uses IAuthoredActivityDefinition/ActivityWorkerSelection and original observed effect contracts, not concrete remote types or guessed numeric JSON references. No new persistent state, codec, scheduler, model, timer or compatibility path.

All other pending plans still pass their actual provider-owned replacement checks. Every unearned active execution, current geometry/state/stock/claims, recurring source and other reference owner still passes original Upgrades.Check. A separate paused upgrade using a DIFFERENT worker is deliberately admitted, then correctly blocks the first plan with core.wrong-target/no order/no travel; external PostOrder preview also refuses without mutation. Saving this refusal produces identical hash with no lingering activation alias. Only an ordinary explicit cancellation of the competing plan permits activation. Existing PlanReferenceReplacementTests additionally exercise native/custom target/worker schemas, preserved intent fields, typed refusals and malformed rewrite faults187/0. No full guard was removed merely to get the new case green.

### Reproduced defects and architectural disposition

The existing offline physical controller case first passes50/0 on2.687. A new two-human-peer flow using the same authored controller/physical construction factory fails4/1: current Controller.Autopilot changes NetSession.Needs, so a delayed valid frame from the original human-owned seat is rejected as Broken. Reading immutable original session seat ownership fixes this path, but the next candidate2.688 still fails11/1 at common step75: original transport allocates human ticket3 while reattached transport starts at4 because native autopilot already allocated3 in the shared seat counter. Equal visible plans/materials hide unequal authoritative command identity. Neither a weaker hash comparison nor skipping takeover is acceptable.

Implementation decision within the authorized contract/no-legacy framework revision: command identity is now **(Player, Stream, Sequence)**. A stable Code identifies the producer; public seat input owns `engine.player-input`, autonomous controller input uses selected module ID `sim.player-controllers`, and scenario pressure uses `sim.scenario-pressure`. Additional selected modules may use their own module ID through explicit World.Submit. There is one authoritative sequence/pending owner: existing CommandInbox. Its per-seat ordinal stream dictionary replaces the old scalar counter; no second inbox/allocator, numeric partition, magic offset, cached-max workaround or compatibility alias/reader.

World.Submit now requires an explicit stream. Missing/unselected streams refuse before allocation. Native command copies and results retain the stream; queued-command and sequence codecs save it, reject malformed/unknown/nonpositive/duplicate/noncanonical identities, and exact duplicate stamped tickets refuse before any write. Existing time/rotating-seat fairness remains; ordinal stream is the deterministic tie-breaker before within-stream sequence. Producer counters are separate from local transport's unadmitted player proposals; network proposal identity never becomes autonomous input authority.

Native controller and pressure systems submit/correlate/validate their own streams, so another producer's same-number reply cannot settle their pending ticket or keep the wrong plan alive. Attached human decision contexts and TestKit answer lookup select the player stream; public result facts retain Stream for hosts to distinguish all company activity. Normal handlers, command ownership, previews, module resource rules and physical timing remain unchanged. Control/pressure providers must still return fresh unstamped commands, now also with no preselected stream.

NetSession derives participation from original saved WorldSettings.Seats, not current play mode. An original human-owned connection remains required during temporary autopilot and after save/reattachment. Initial autonomous seats cannot claim a local transport slot; failed attachment leaves Net/clock/state untouched. This is a transport ownership/configuration rule, not a different gameplay refusal when a host uses networking. Self-review removed a candidate CheckController refusal based on local Clock.Sequenced: that would diverge from transport-free replay and must not enter authoritative game rules. The source test instead verifies identical pure gameplay previews/checks after attachment. No disconnection migration, reassignment of initially autonomous ownership, peer transfer or reconnect policy is implemented here.

Network admission additionally validates positive/increasing within-frame player tickets and preflights every seat at an epoch before committing any. A regressing delayed ticket yields NetState.Broken without partially admitting earlier seats or escaping Update through the inbox duplicate guard. Reordered valid future frames remain allowed. No packet interpretation, membership decision, sequence repair or game rule moves into the shared test Pair.

Save161 and replay2 deliberately replace the former formats; final engine build identity is2.690; earlier2.689 candidates are superseded and cannot be mixed with the final build. Controller/pressure system schema versions become2. Replay inputs remain the external player stream and autonomous inputs are recomputed by the engine; replay checkpoints now include producer-scoped result identity. Existing native recorder/verifier owns this codec, not a new replay implementation. Old saves/replays/builds remain incompatible and untouched. Never rewrite their headers, including the completed2.682 marine artifacts.

### Actual public networked controller flow

Existing PlayerControllerTests.Factory authors the same point-space playbook, template, worker roles, material stock and timed construction. Two genuine human-seat peers submit a normal worker policy and normal human→autopilot intent through the same Pair. Both sessions then admit human takeover through transport; dormant controller decision counts stop while physical work continues. Real result facts include player ticket1 and controller ticket1 as distinct identities, with no duplicate full identity. Save the human-controlled state, reattach again, resume native autopilot and finish both physical towers with exactly4 of20 source material left. Additional updates create no duplicate plans/work. Three canonical replay pairs verify four full external inputs, one pending-controller-to-takeover input and one takeover-to-final input; autonomous proposals are regenerated, not recorded twice. Original/restored peers converge with actual delayed/reversed batches. New case38/0 uses only commands, views, clock updates, saves and existing engine-owned content; it adds no host AI.

New source contract case40/0 directly exercises the real inbox/result codecs as a unit/fault-injection boundary: three selected streams each own ticket1, pending saves/continuations and ordinal results agree, wrong producers/duplicates refuse purely,64 concurrent allocations remain unique, and malformed sequence maps reject. This unit fixture is not a replacement gameplay runtime or a claim that arbitrary injected autonomous commands can be replayed without their engine producer.

### Current implementation and self-review

One existing `NetworkedModDurabilityTests.Pair` remains the byte transport owner. Explicit observation/diagnostic callbacks allow distinct authored games without importing colony-specific assumptions. Local seats0/1 still use native lockstep, input delay2/checksum every step, delayed batches reversed every2/3 rounds, original hello/result/command/save/hash/replay owners. No packet decoding, direct mutable World access, fabricated peer frame or host-side game decision is added. Two scenario files are partials of that same test class, not parallel transport/game runtimes.

The actual new automation flow exposed a pacing defect in the proof driver, not an engine defect: an Update after stalled input can consume several already-earned steps. Checking only before Update cannot enforce a committed-epoch ceiling and can miss a one-step pending decision. Pair now sets the **existing public local observation budget** `Clock.CatchUpCap=1` and checks that no Update advances more than one step. This does not cap scenario size/time, discard earned backlog, add a clock, change production pacing defaults, bypass pause via RunTicks, or weaken lockstep. Every intermediate world publication still comes from ordinary Game.Update. The engine remains free to use other host budgets.

`WeatherIncidentPropagationTests.Factory` and `CompanyAlertAutomationTests.Factory` become internal for reuse; their JSON composition/behavior is unchanged. New reads use immutable views and the existing entity helper. Automation locates physical work through `IncidentsView.TryGetResponseOrder`, never a guessed plan-key convention. Profile branches are copied from engine metadata; no rule evaluation is implemented in the test. Native rules/alerts/work/incidents own every gameplay transition. No production source or proof-module gameplay implementation changed in M01.

### Actual new flow W: weather and connected response

Two peers admit one owner incident and one simultaneous foreign Codes.NotOwned refusal, then save the actual first dry pending exposure with original8-minute delay. Reattach another peer pair, network-pause the originals at actual step7 and advance the saved session only to due+1/step12. Originals retain one receipt and a frozen hash; saved peers observe actual wet weather and cancel it without target damage. Resuming originals reaches the identical step12 hash `1AA348FCD15B49DA`. The retained original dry observation is detached.

Both pairs then earn a new dry exposure with a fresh full8-minute delay; only the connected target gets episode1. Real hauling loads one finite water unit, saves loaded cargo, delivers and completes timed source repair; a second real haul supplies target repair. Depot10 becomes8, total journal consumption2, truck/source/target residual water0, remote target remains inactive. The invariant checks stock plus original resource-flow journal consumption on every publication. Pending/loaded/settled saves and three canonical replay segments (4 full inputs,1 pending-to-loaded,1 loaded-to-settled) reproduce exact final hashes. Actual stalls and reversed byte batches occur in original and restored sessions.

### Actual new flow A: deferred emergency automation

Two separate human-seat peers configure the existing JSON company-alert rule, refuse the same foreign binding and admit an actual incident. Authored dwell produces PendingDecision=true while alert is still Normal, stock20 and damage active. Its saved PendingAt names the next **unexecuted** engine step: after publication it equals Clock.StepIndex, not StepIndex+1. Reattached twins apply the normal engine company command on their next step, yielding exactly alert version1/Emergency without granting repair/materials.

Network-pause the original applied-emergency pair at step15; another saved pair advances to19 with its own actual time, while originals retain hash `EBFE2A95826C46B9`. Resume/converge at a common epoch. Ordinary worker policy enables the compatible machine; actual dispatch offers contain authored emergency priority+500. Native travel and supplied repair consume8 materials and earn partial productive work with that exact worker before completion. Save partial work/reservations. The healthy signal later earns PendingDecision=false while Emergency remains applied; save/reattach that pending state and observe the next engine step restore Normal/version2. Eight additional actual steps produce no repeated repair, alert commit or material effects; final stock12. Company1 alert remains version0 and cannot read company0 rule on every publication.

Four peer-canonical replay pairs independently verify full4 inputs, pending-emergency-to-partial-repair1 input, partial-repair-to-pending-normal0 inputs, and pending-normal-to-settled0 inputs against their exact corresponding hashes. All four advanced final worlds converge. No direct repair, automatic policy trigger or material consumption is implemented outside engine contracts.

final71/0. Original P6.acceptance.009 and other broad durability requirements remain

## Inspected boundary and reused ownership

Current SimGame.World is already internal, with only framework Tests granted friend
access. PlayerDecisionContext exposes scoped views/commands and no lifecycle,
observer or mutable world handle. Those inspected paths needed no new change.
This is not a fresh audit of every possible view/module/host boundary.

The new proof reuses ComposedModProofTests' same16-module factory, authored JSON
and pure view/invariant readers. Those test helpers changed visibility from private
to internal only; their simulation behavior/content did not change. No duplicate
game model, mod runtime, inventory, phase owner or game rules were introduced.

Pair holds two real SimGame/NetSession instances. It submits through the local human
seat and forwards original byte arrays through DrainOutgoing/Receive, delaying and
reversing actual batches. It decodes/applies no packets or commands. Native lockstep
gates every attempted Game.Update and compares checksums every committed step.
Input delay2/checksum interval1 are explicit transport options, not a second clock.

The faster host is paced until its peer reaches that epoch. Independent pairs may
observe a milestone at different publications, so the driver advances only the
lower pair to the same actual game-clock step before comparing state. No world,
route, resource, control, job, inventory or time field is written directly.

## Executed durability sequence

1. Two original peers have distinct local human seats0/1. Seat0 proposes depot
   reserve and physical haul8; seat1 simultaneously proposes a forbidden foreign
   policy change. Before input admission, recorder count0. After real partial load,
   all3 commands have native results, accepted owner actions and identical
   Codes.NotFound foreign refusal. Replay is canonical on both peers.
2. Save both original peers at a committed partial-loading epoch, load two new games
   and reattach their native lockstep sessions. New sessions use original codecs,
   source inventories, worker/handling progress and last admitted command sequences.
   Handshake checks actual content/build/settings/command/state identity.
3. Original pair network-pauses through RequestPause and actual frames. Advance
   restored pair while originals remain clock/hash frozen, proving real active
   isolation rather than only creating an untouched session. Original RequestResume
   is transported normally. Catch up to restored committed epoch and compare hash.
4. Both independent peer pairs physically finish supply and return home. Submit
   the same new project intent to each; next command sequence matches after load.
   Real supplied fabricator work is observed and checkpointed, then facility
   completion occurs through the original graph/constructible/site owners.
5. Each pair submits worker hold, an unsupported incident request (typed refusal),
   and a valid incident. Real detection reaches WorkResourceCodes.Stock. Finish and
   independently verify the mid-load replay segment, whose initial save has a
   nonzero simulation step and whose4 new commands include project/incident inputs.
6. Load a replacement restored pair from the original blocked-incident saves and
   reattach lockstep again. Normal release-reserve and worker-release proposals
   continue as native seat0 sequences7/8, not restarted or duplicated identities.
   Physical resupply and repair earn partial progress and checkpoint; restart and
   worker return complete with original total2 steel, empty endpoint/signal0.
7. All four final peers agree. Real transport stalls and reversed multi-packet
   batches are observed in original and final restored sessions. Three pairs of
   recorder streams are byte-identical between peers and independently replay to
   their exact corresponding current authoritative hash:

   - Full initial session:9 admitted commands, including foreign/unsupported refusals.
   - Mid-load save segment:4 commands, ending at blocked incident.
   - Blocked-incident save segment:2 commands, ending after actual recovery/return.

Every round uses the shared original mod invariant reader to check real inventory
totals12/4/2, actual typed signal equals endpoint inventory and no physical overlap.
No precomputed gameplay result or test-side resource repair is used.

## Save and comparison boundary

Checkpoints occur after submitted commands are admitted/answered and no scripted
command remains outstanding. Reattachment reconstructs transport from that native
committed state. This proof does not claim simulation saves persist external
packets, unadmitted network proposals or the previous transport's buffered state.
Hashes compare the same game-clock epoch; a different wall-clock publication is
not a game-state mismatch by itself. Retained presentation caches are not introduced
into authoritative save/hash state to make the comparison pass.
